# ARC-AGI-3 Iteration Storyline (for cross-AI evaluation)

A chronological narrative of every iteration we've attempted on the
**arc-prize-2026-arc-agi-3** Kaggle competition. Read top-to-bottom to follow
the decision tree, regressions, and current open questions.

---

## 0. Project context

- **Competition**: Kaggle "ARC Prize 2026 — ARC-AGI-3" (interactive reasoning benchmark)
- **Deadline**: 2026-11-02
- **Scoring metric**: **RHAE** (Random Human-Action Efficiency) — per level it's `(human_actions / agent_actions)^2`, summed/averaged across 25 public games. Displayed score is a **percentage** (so "0.43" on the LB = 0.43%, "1.30" = 1.30%).
- **Game shape**: Agent controls a player via ACTION1-7 (4 directional + 1 interact + 1 click `(x,y)` + 1 undo) on 64×64 grids with 16 discrete colors. 25 public games × N levels each.
- **Kaggle environment**: Code-competition. Notebook reruns inside Kaggle: ALL 25 games run in parallel threads sharing CPU. ~8 hours per game wall-clock. **GPU OOMs with 25 parallel CNNs**, so production env is effectively CPU-only.
- **Repo location**: `f:/kaggle/arc-prize-2026/`
- **Notebook slug**: `canivel/arc3-final` (each "vNN" below = kernel version #)
- **Best score so far**: 0.43% (single lucky draw; mean ~0.29 on our champion code)
- **Current #1 on LB**: Tufa Labs at **1.30%** (3-4× above next tier)

---

## 1. Pre-eval-harness era (Apr-May 2026)

We submitted >15 daily variants of a BFS + CNN agent (the **FORGE / v35 / v39 family**), scoring all over the 0.17-0.33 range with no clear pattern. The key insight that took us months to internalize:

> **Public LB single-submission noise is ≥0.21 wide on IDENTICAL code.** v31=0.43 and v32=0.22 on the literal same kernel proved it.

Highlights from this era:

| Date | Kernel | Code description | Kaggle |
|------|--------|------------------|--------|
| 2026-05-06 | v22 | BFS + CLTI demos, `sel.set_data` fix for ACTION6 | **0.30** |
| 2026-05-12 | v27 | v22 + Blind-Squirrel back-label win-distance | 0.25 |
| 2026-05-13 | v28 | v22 + AXIOM-lite object-centric verb dict | 0.19 |
| 2026-05-15 | v30 | v22 + offline-pretrained ForgeNet (507 BFS demos) | 0.22 |
| 2026-05-16 | v15 | **v35** = per-level BFS→GraphExplorer hybrid (memory's "breakthrough") | local sweep showed 39 levels vs v22=36 |
| 2026-05-19 | v17/19 | v39 = same engine + correct env (`enable_gpu:true` + BYOD docker `kaggle-private-byod@sha256:00377cd1...`) | 0.24 |
| 2026-05-24 | v23 | v44 = v39 + TTT-step-1 (CNN persists across levels in a game) | 0.20 |
| 2026-05-26 | v27 | "v46" = v39 baseline revert (after v45 error) | **0.33** |
| 2026-05-27 | v28 | v46 variance check, SAME code | 0.24 |

The 0.33 → 0.24 swing on identical code (one-day apart) was the moment we admitted public-LB iteration is statistically useless.

---

## 2. Critical recurring bugs we hit and fixed

### 2a. ACTION6 `set_data` bug (memorialized)

`GameAction.ACTION6` defaults to `(x=0, y=0)` when sent via the gateway unless you call `sel.set_data({"x":x, "y":y})`. Multiple versions stored coords in `self._last_action_data` but never invoked `set_data`. **Every CNN-driven click went to (0,0)**. Confirmed via `arcengine` source. v22 was the first version to fix it cleanly.

### 2b. Poisoned pretrained ForgeNet weights

Discovered 2026-05-31: the `canivel/forge-pretrained-weights` dataset was trained on data collected BEFORE the set_data fix existed. The CNN learned click coordinates that "only work" when clipped to (0,0). Once we fixed the agent to actually click at the predicted (x,y), the pretrained CNN's coordinates were garbage → catastrophic regression (**v30 → 0.04**). Fix: **don't load pretrained ForgeNet** (random init, learn online). Result: v31=0.43 (lucky draw), v32=0.22 (same code variance).

### 2c. `scored.sort(reverse=True)` dict-comparison bug

`v39_agent.py` line 1538 had `scored.append((score, act_id, data))` followed by `scored.sort(reverse=True)`. When `score` ties, Python tries to compare the `data` dicts → `TypeError`. The outer `try/except` in `choose_action` catches it → random fallback action. Silent degradation on every novelty-sampling call. Fixed: `scored.sort(key=lambda t: (t[0], t[1]), reverse=True)`.

### 2d. PYTHONHASHSEED salt

Python's builtin `hash(str)` is PYTHONHASHSEED-salted per process. Earlier versions used `hash(game_id)` for the per-game seed — every Kaggle submission was a fresh random draw. v37 fixed it with `int(hashlib.md5(str(game_id).encode())[:8], 16)`. After this fix, local results became fully reproducible.

---

## 3. The JEPA-WM detour (2026-05-24 to 2026-05-26)

User asked for a "Tufa-class" world model. We built the full **JEPA (Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture)** stack in a single session:

- `jepa_wm/models/jepa.py` — ViT-S encoder (12L d=256), EMA target encoder, action-conditioned predictor. **25.64M params, ~98MB ckpt.**
- `jepa_wm/inference/mcts.py` — Latent-space PUCT MCTS with batched dynamics.
- `jepa_wm/data/gen_trajectories.py` — BFS replay across 25 games + 400 random actions/game → **10,188 (s_t, a, s_t+1, r, done) tuples**, saved as `jepa_wm/data/trajectories.npz`.
- `jepa_wm/training/train.py` — EMA target encoder + L1 latent loss + reward/done heads + VICReg variance reg.
- `jepa_wm/inference/agent_hooks.py` — `JEPAHook.pick_action(frame, available_actions, click_candidates)` for v45 integration.

**Training**: 20k steps, batch 32 × grad_accum 4, AMP. Final r-loss 0.175, latent_var 1.63 (no collapse). ~2.5h on RTX 3080.

**Integration v45 = v39 + JEPAHook for BFS-failed levels.**

### Submitted as kernel v26 (2026-05-26 00:15 UTC). **ERRORED on rerun.**

Diagnosis (later, via local load-test of 25 parallel processes):

- **9.9GB RAM** just for 25 × 400MB JEPA worker copies (Kaggle's parallel env has ~13GB total)
- **41 seconds per MCTS pick** at the original budget (32 sims, depth 8) — Kaggle agent gateway has ~10s per-action timeout
- JEPA at 25M params just won't fit Kaggle's CPU+parallel rerun

Concluded: **need a ViT-XXS distillation (~3-4M params)** before retrying JEPA. Deferred indefinitely.

---

## 4. Building the local eval harness (the game-changer, 2026-05-28)

Public LB noise made A/B impossible. So we built `eval_harness.py`:

- For each of 25 games: instantiate the game class directly via `importlib`, bypass the HTTP gateway, run `agent.choose_action()` loop bounded by `--budget` actions and `--bfs-s` BFS-time cap.
- Records: `levels_completed`, `actions_per_level`, terminal state (WIN / GAME_OVER / BUDGET), wall time.
- Supports A/B compare mode (two agent .py files side-by-side).
- BFSSolver monkey-patch enforces `bfs_timeout` cap regardless of agent's internal adaptive logic.

**Determinism verified**: ran v39 baseline twice (replicate 1 vs replicate 2), 0 game-level differences. The hashlib seed fix makes everything reproducible.

### Baseline results (budget=200, bfs-s=15):

| Agent | total_levels | games_with_progress | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **v39 baseline** (BFS + CNN) | **12** | 11/25 | dict-sort fix applied |
| v44 (CNN persist across levels) | 12 | 11/25 | IDENTICAL to v39 — CNN-persist invisible at 200-action budget |
| **v35 (per-level BFS→GE hybrid)** | **19** | 13/25 | **+7 vs v39.** Gains: bp35 +1, cd82 +2, r11l +1, tu93 +4. Loss: ar25 -1. |
| **v35 @ bfs-s=30** (more BFS time) | **25** | 13/25, 1 WIN | **+13 vs v39.** Also unlocked sk48 (0→1), tu93 (5→9). First terminal WIN. |
| v37 (v35 + per-level wall-clock deadline) | 20 | 11/25 | LESS than v35 |
| v47 (PURE SG port, no BFS) | **2** | 2/25 | Catastrophic. Needs 8h/game for online CNN to actually train. |
| v48 (v39 BFS + SG-CNN cold replacing GE) | **11** | 11/25 | WORSE than v39. SG-CNN cold-start can't replace GE at 200-action budget. |

Probed the 14 zero-solve games at budget=400, bfs-s=60 → **ALL stayed at 0**. These games (g50t, ka59, lf52, re86, sb26, sc25, su15, tn36, tr87, wa30, etc.) are structurally beyond BFS+GE. They have arm/unlock mechanics where actions produce zero visible frame-change → search has nothing to traverse.

**The 25-level local ceiling = our BFS+GE architecture's hard ceiling.** Breaking past it requires:
- Learned dynamics (JEPA / world models) — deferred (too heavy for Kaggle rerun)
- Object-centric (AXIOM) — tested in v28 standalone, got 0% on these games
- LLM-driven code synthesis (Rodionov-style) — out of scope for 1-2 day builds

## 5. The "1.30 score mystery" research arc (2026-06-03 to 2026-06-10)

User reported: "someone scored 1.30 — investigate".

### 5a. Deep-research dive

Spawned multi-agent workflow (98 sub-agents, 2.6M tokens, 50min) to find the technique:

- **Tufa Labs / StochasticGoose (Dries Smit)** = current #1 on main LB at 1.30. Advised by **Jack Cole (MindsAI)** — MindsAI is the team famous for TTT (test-time training) on ARC-AGI-1 and 2.
- Earlier Preview Competition: Tufa scored 12.58% RHAE (which displays as 12.58% on Preview LB, or some smaller number on main LB).
- Public Tufa code: **github.com/DriesSmit/ARC3-solution/custom_agents/action.py** (489 lines).
- Architecture: **16-channel one-hot 64×64 input → 4-layer CNN backbone (32→64→128→256) → action head (5 logits ACTION1-5) + FULLY-CONVOLUTIONAL 64×64 coord head (4096 logits ACTION6)** → unified 4101-logit output. BCE loss on `(state, action) → frame_changed`. 200K hash-dedup buffer. Reset model + buffer on level-up. NO BFS, NO MCTS, NO symbolic search. ~100 lines of PyTorch.
- **Key architectural insight**: BFS/GraphExplorer can't discriminate which ACTION6 click coords are even LEGAL on a 64×64 grid. SG's fully-conv coord head learns exactly that.
- Other notable verified techniques:
  - **Rodionov "Executable World Models"** (arXiv 2605.05138, 32.58% mean RHAE) — Codex CLI + GPT-5.4 synthesizes a refactorable Python world model. Best technique overall but needs LLM Code-CLI integration, not 1-2 day work.
  - **Rudakov graph-explorer** (arXiv 2512.24156, 3rd in Preview) — same architecture family as our v35. Confirms v35 lineage is sound.
  - **Duke LRM harness** — 97.1% on TR87 but 0% on BP35. Doesn't generalize. Out.

### 5b. Pure-SG port → kernel v34

- File: `notebooks/forge_agent/v47_agent.py` (~250 lines, faithful port of the SG repo)
- Local at budget=200, bfs-s=15: **2 levels** — confirmed SG architecture needs 8h game wall-clock for online CNN to train.
- **Shipped anyway** (Tufa's 1.30 validates the architecture, just needs the bigger budget).
- **Kaggle v34 = 0.18.** Within v39 noise floor but no breakthrough.
- Interpretation: my pure-SG matches the public Preview-era SG, but Tufa's main-comp 1.30 has more than the public repo.

### 5c. SG + pretrained CNN → kernel v36 (DISASTER)

- Hypothesis: SG's random-init CNN wastes most of the 8h on cold learning. Pretraining on our existing 10k BFS trajectories should warm it up.
- Built `train_sg_pretrain.py`: loads `jepa_wm/data/trajectories.npz`, computes `frame_changed` labels, trains SG `ActionModel` for 20k steps with BCE loss.
- **Final val acc 0.95.** Shipped weights as Kaggle dataset `canivel/sg-pretrained-weights` (131MB).
- Patched v47 → v49 to load weights at level-reset.
- **Kaggle v36 = 0.01.** CATASTROPHIC.

**Root cause**: The 10k trajectories are **78.8% positive labels** (`frame_changed=1`) because they come from BFS solutions = sequences of productive actions. CNN learned to predict `1` for almost everything → overconfident in wrong directions → worse than random init.

### 5d. Class-balanced SG retrain → kernel v37

- Added `pos_weight = (1-pos_frac)/pos_frac = 0.27` to the BCE loss to counter the bias.
- Re-trained 20k steps. Final acc 0.947 (vs 0.953 unbalanced — small drop, expected).
- Uploaded as v2 of `canivel/sg-pretrained-weights`.
- **Kaggle v37 = 0.22.** Recovered to v35-baseline range. Class balancing did fix the catastrophe. But still nowhere near Tufa's 1.30.

### 5e. BFS + balanced-pretrained-SG hybrid → kernel v38 (PENDING)

- File: `notebooks/forge_agent/v50_agent.py` = v48 (BFS + SG hybrid) + the v49 pretrained-weight loading code path.
- Logic: BFS first for any BFS-solvable level → SG-CNN with warm pretrained weights for BFS-failed levels.
- Just submitted 2026-06-10 13:56 UTC. Awaiting score.

## 8. Things we KNOW work for someone but we can't (yet) implement

- **Test-Time Training (TTT) on a pretrained transformer** — MindsAI's signature. Tufa's likely secret. Requires a pretrained foundation model that adapts during the 8h game window via gradient updates on rolling experience.
- **Executable World Models** (Rodionov) — Codex CLI synthesizes a Python game model at test time. 32.58% mean RHAE, 7/25 games fully solved. Out of our 1-2 day reach.
- **LLM-driven Python history-retrieval harnesses** (Duke) — 97.1% on TR87 but 0% on BP35. Wins specific games, doesn't generalize. Tactical option.

---

## 9. Open questions for the next AI to weigh in on

1. **The bias correction was the easy fix; what about the data itself?** Our `trajectories.npz` is BFS solutions + 400 random actions/game. The class-balanced pretrain gets us back to baseline but not above. Is the data fundamentally wrong for SG-style supervised learning, or do we need MORE data, or a different label signal?

2. **What's Tufa's actual extra ingredient?** Public SG (Preview-era) ≈ our v34 (0.18). Tufa main-comp 1.30 = 7× our number. The gap is too big for "they trained longer". What architectural addition gets 7× from public SG?

3. **Should we attempt the Rodionov LLM-code-synthesis path?** That's literally proven to work (32.58% mean RHAE) but requires Codex CLI inside a Kaggle code-comp notebook. Feasible?

4. **The JEPA dead-end revisit**: we shelved it because 25M params × 25 parallel agents = 9.9GB RAM, too tight. If we distill to a ViT-XXS (~3-4M params), it fits. Worth revisiting now that we've spent a week on SG?

5. **What does the v38 hybrid result (pending) tell us?** If v38 > 0.30, hybrid stacks; ~0.25 means they're redundant; <0.15 means SG-CNN hurts the BFS path.

6. **The 14 unreachable zero-solve games**: g50t, ka59, lf52, re86, sb26, sc25, su15, tn36, tr87, wa30 (and a few others). What single mechanism cracks them? Memory/state we're not tracking? Hidden game-rule discovery?

7. **Sample efficiency on Kaggle vs local**: our local 25 → Kaggle 0.31 mapping suggests 1 local level ≈ 0.012 Kaggle RHAE points. Tufa's 1.30 = ~108 "local levels equivalent". Where do those come from?

- **The four FATALs that changed the plan:** (1) **wall clock, not actions, is the binding resource** — all 50 game-runs across two pulls ended at ~7,920 s vs `max_runtime_s_per_game=7920.0` with `max_actions_per_game=None` (rl-planning + systems, independently) ⇒ §2.3's rationale false on our rail, and Δlc **cannot separate "harmful" from "slower"**; (2) **inherited non-harm gate fails a true null 51.1%** (methodology, 90 null-vs-null pairs in `runs/null10`; mean-leg 12.2%, worst-game leg **50.0%**); (3) **S1/L0 would measure a degenerate constant** — **0 of 25 sims implement abstention** so Tycho "coverage" ≡ 1.0, only 3 sims hold state, gate unreachable and circular (prog-synthesis; independently corroborated by the runnability check) — plus **the "91.7% held-out" was never held out** (`split=all`); (4) **P1's mechanism is contradicted by ~6 strings in our own harness** (`prompts.py:80/82/107/113`, `_PYTHON_TOOL_DESCRIPTION`, the `code` JSON schema) ⇒ **K4 can pass validly but cannot fail validly**; `SAFE_MODULES` also lacks `dataclasses`/`typing`/`enum`, so **the Tycho `State` dataclass is not constructible in our sandbox**.
- **POST-PANEL OVERTURN — A22 compaction death record VACATED (verified by me, not taken from an agent).** The recalibration reproduced every null digit (12.22 / 50.00 / 51.11%, null sd 0.1223) and then found: **`war_eval_v1/v2/v3` are three runs of the IDENTICAL warpack config** (same label/solver/n_passes, **byte-identical `git_status.txt`**, 07-14/15/16) scoring **22 / 15 / 13 lc**. I recomputed it myself: **v3−v1 = mean Δlc −0.360, worst −2 — bit-for-bit A22 v2.1's headline "harm", with no compaction in either run** (that pair's sign-flip p=0.0195 beats v2.1's own 0.0781). Warpack run variance is **4.83× vanilla** (p=0.038), so `null10` understates the warpack null; and A22 was screened against `war_eval_v1`, the **22-level high outlier** of its own family (flagged as a lucky draw back on 07-16). Re-baselined on the 3-run mean: **v1 +0.013, v2 −0.107, v2.1 −0.147 — all PASS.** Provenance kicker: **−0.128 is an arithmetic identity, not a statistic** — (12−15.2)/25, sourced from a 12-level arm and applied against a 22-level one that itself screens **+0.272, p=0.0074**. **Ruling: A22 is UNRESOLVED, not DEAD** — three strikes by a broken instrument against a lucky baseline. This does **not** resurrect the lane by default (lane (a) was ratified on independent grounds and keeps the budget), but **no lane may be declared dead on the old gate**, and "eviction is intrinsically harmful" is **no longer evidenced by our own data**. §2.2 monotonicity falls outright (steps z −0.99/−0.33; ρ shift p=0.329).
- **K3′ SEALED, effective immediately:** pair against the per-game mean of **m ≥ 3 same-config baseline runs**; PASS iff `mean Δlc ≥ −t(0.95,df=m−1)·s_base·√(1+1/m)`; fixed fallbacks **−0.200 at m=1** (2.2% type-I), −0.190 at m≥3, −0.160 at α=0.10. **Worst-game leg DROPPED** (structurally uninformative at 25 games; only −3 reaches α=0.05). Reviewer's pairing-inflation claim corrected: true inflation **1.28×**, not 1.78× ⇒ the defect was primarily the *number*, not only the procedure. **Cost imposed: every future arm needs m ≥ 3 same-config baselines before it can be screened at all, and a warpack-specific null is owed.** Artifacts: `learnings/sweeps/gate_recalibration_2026-08-09.md`, `runs/gate_recalibration_2026-08-09.json`.

  - **The six §5.2 items, resolved with evidence before the run.** (i) **No coverage channel exists** — `coverage_strict` measured at exactly 1.0 (0 sim_errors, 0 selfdiffs over 4,996 banked steps) and the identity-abstention proxy is computed from the observed label, so it is circular *and* mechanically inflates `accepted_match` (an identity-on-change step is necessarily a mismatch). - **K3-prime IS ITSELF MISCALIBRATED — superseded by K3-double-prime within 24 h of being sealed.** R25 methodology N1 **reproduced**: the sealed m=3 line is **−0.2916** against its own m=1 fallback of **−0.200** — 46% *looser* at more baselines, which is backwards. Cause: an empirical m=1 quantile (2.326·s) spliced onto a parametric t-interval (3.372·s at m=3); the driver is t(0.95,2)=2.920, not the sqrt(1+1/m) term. The **−0.190 m>=3 fallback is not derivable from the formula at all**. Measured type-I drifts 2.0% → 7.1% (m=1→3) on 400k bootstrap; 4.6% of draws degenerate. **K3-double-prime: `mean dlc >= −C(m)·sigma-hat`, C = 2.33/2.10/2.02/1.98/1.96/1.94, sigma-hat pooled at df>=4** — monotone by construction; type-I 2.0/4.4/4.8/5.2%.

An independent re-derivation (own reader, validated by exactly reproducing `actions_per_level` for all 25 games, the `act` column, dup=117 and re-traversal=180) swept **~1,000 rule variants** — batch keying, trigger set, dead scope, visited scope, level attribution, RESET handling, precedence — and **nothing yields 195**; nearest is **185** (|Δ|=10), and the P1 validator's *other* code path (`baseline_stats`) already independently reports **185 / 16.67%**. The 101-vs-195 gap was **mostly definitional but not entirely**: both share the dead-batch rule and the scope (17 cleared levels, 1,110 actions) and differ only in precedence over bucket (b) — the validator gives duplicate `(s,a)` precedence so buckets **partition** (⇒ **101**, the *marginal* saving of batch-abort over the memo), the diagnosis lets them **overlap** (⇒ **185** gross, not 195). **Neither number answers "preventable actions on cleared levels" — that is the union, 218 = 19.6%.** Root cause: column (a) was computed as `act − b − c` over overlapping buckets, so the **84-action overlap was subtracted twice**, inflating "removable" and deflating "necessary". Corrections (prose only): removable **312 (28%) → 218 (19.6%)**, necessary **798 (72%) → 892 (80.4%)**, tail **195/17.6% → 185/16.67%**, ar25 L1 **117→69**, sp80 **60→53**. **UNAFFECTED: the ×1.10 / +0.184 arithmetic** — the scoring path removed each action exactly once (1.8188 published vs 1.8239 re-derived; double-counting would have given 1.8414) — and **no sealed P1 endpoint depends on 195**. A **correction notice was prepended to `efficiency_diagnosis_2026-08-12.md` with the body left as originally written**, so the record shows what was claimed and when rather than being silently rewritten.

#### 2026-08-13 — ★★★ P2 (VERIFIED-PLAN BATCH GATING) — BUILT, ENGINE-TESTED, **REFUSED AT THE PRE-PUSH REPLAY. SLOT 2 NOT SPENT.**

Record: `learnings/war_room/p2_prereg_2026-08-13.md` (pre-registration **and** the refusal, sealed together). Module `duck_eval/warpack/_kaggle_dataset/p2_batchgate_patch.py` v1 (4 seams, house pattern, **flag-gated OFF, never pushed**), replay `duck_eval/warpack/p2_replay.py` (30 rules × 4 recorded runs, shipped detectors + shipped predicate + exact scorer) → `runs/p2_replay/sweep.json`, smoke `duck_eval/warpack/p2_smoke.py` **42 passed / 0 failed** incl. real-`arcengine` integration.

- **★★★ THE CAP AS BRIEFED DELETES LEVEL COMPLETIONS — the brief's own hard stop fired.** cap=1 cuts **5 level-completing actions** across **ar25, sp80, tu93** — the exact three batches the brief warned about; cap=2 also 5, cap=3 → 4, cap=4 → 3, cap=6 → 2. Abort-on-first-revisit cuts 4 (this is `P1_ABORT_REVISIT`, already refused 08-12 for the same reason).
- **★★★ THE APPARENT GAIN OF A CAP IS AN ARTEFACT OF THE REPLAY. New standing rule: publish the LC-HONEST multiplier.** A naive replay holds `levels_completed` fixed, so it credits the cap with removing actions from a level *the cap just prevented from completing*. Re-scoring the lost level (and every deeper one) as incomplete: **cap=1 goes ×1.0574 → ×0.9457**; cap=2 ×1.0349 → ×0.9397; cap=4 ×1.0173 → ×0.9440. **Every lc-cutting rule is net NEGATIVE.** `p2_replay.py` now emits both columns by construction.
- **★★★ THE PREMISE DOES NOT SURVIVE THE HARNESS: THERE IS NO 11-ACTION BLIND BATCH.** B4 = 11.11 is actions per **ANALYSIS TURN**; a cap can only cap a **BATCH** (one `step_env`, one tool call). **79.1–85.4% of batches are a SINGLE action, median 1, mean 1.64–2.20**; batches ≥5 are 5.0–10.4% of batches (29.9–51.3% of actions). The agent reaches ~11 by making **1.6–2.6 sequential tool calls per analysis turn** — and **the harness returns the board to it after every one**. It is not firing blind; it is **persisting deliberately across informed observations**. "Blind-batch tail" is the wrong name for the object.
- **★★ AND THE LONG STALL-GATED BATCHES ARE WHERE THE LEVEL COMPLETIONS LIVE.** In **all four** recorded instances the completing action is the **LAST** action of a long stall-gated batch that OPENS with re-traversal: tu93 L1 (size 16, lc @16, gate `nz=51`), ar25 L1 (15, lc @15, `nz=110`), sp80 L1 animation (4, lc @4, `nz=19,rev=4`), sp80 L1 effnote (6, lc @6, `nz=31`). Any position cap N destroys every winning batch longer than N; ar25/tu93 need N ≥ 15.
- **The safe redesign is built and parked, not pushed.** k-tolerant **STALE-STATE RUN cap** (abort once k consecutive actions reach no board state new to this level; a no-op is a strict special case). LC cut = 0 for `noop_run=1` (4.21% saved, **×1.0091**), `stale_run=8` persisted (2.96%, ×1.0117), `stale_run=6` (2.34%, ×1.0029). **Refused a slot for three reasons:** (i) ×1.003–×1.012 is an order of magnitude inside our per-draw sd 0.1513 and the family is **m=2 ⇒ NOT SCREENABLE**; (ii) the best safe rule is a strict **SUBSET of P1 mechanism B**, which already ran live 08-12 (75 aborts, M0 3.68%) and was NO-PROMOTE; (iii) the gate arms on only **6.2–9.2%** of batches and the rule aborts **26–47 times across four whole runs** — and P1's live delivery came in at *half* its replay estimate, so a P2 arm would deliver ~1% on ~6 aborts/run. A foregone null, not a reading.
- **Behavioural endpoint sealed anyway, for whoever returns to this.** **G1 = actions REQUESTED per stall-gated batch** — the cap truncates execution but never shrinks the request, so it cannot be moved mechanically by the mechanism. **Control spread 2.169 – 3.000** (`runs/p2_replay/control_spread.json`); PASS would have required **G1 < 2.169**. Direction of B4 *does* reproduce at batch granularity: **effnote_v1 G1 = 4.490, above the control max** — but the biteable quantity is ~4.5 actions, not 11.
- **Disposition: DO NOT PUSH.** No kernel push, no dataset push, no submission, queue untouched, no cloud spend. **Push usage 2026-08-13: 1 of 2 (EFFNOTE); slot 2 UNSPENT.** Other 11 dataset modules byte-verified unchanged (11/11 sha256 match). Module left in `_kaggle_dataset/` **inert** (`P2_BATCHGATE != 1` ⇒ `apply()` False, seams unchanged, verified in a clean subprocess) so a later slot can arm it without re-deriving anything.
- **What this leaves open.** Not "a bigger k" and not the position cap at any value — both refuted with numbers. The object that is actually there is **deliberate persistence across informed observations**, which is a *turn*-level phenomenon; any runner-side lever on it must run the lc-safety proof first, because every recorded level completion sits at the end of a long stall-gated burst.

- **★★★★ R-7 REFRAMED — THE "EFFICIENCY CEILING" IS A *MEAN*, AND MEANS COMPOUND WITH DRAW COUNT. THE LORA LANE IS A WINNING LANE, NOT A CONSOLATION PRIZE.**
  - The 08-12 diagnosis' "efficiency ceiling ≈1.26–1.36 LB" was computed by re-scoring our OWN runs at human action counts ⇒ it is **what a typical run would score = a per-draw MEAN**, not a max. Our current per-draw mean is **0.9503** (sd 0.151), and our best-of-29 is 1.33.
  - Order statistics on the SAME sd, ~80 draws left to Nov 2 (`E[max]` Blom): **current mean 0.950 → E[max@80] = 1.316** (matches our observed 1.33 — the model is calibrated). **Mean 1.26 → E[max@80] = 1.625. Mean 1.36 → E[max@80] = 1.725.** At k=110: 1.642 / 1.742.
  - **Gold line 1.58. Leader 1.86.** ⇒ **A corpus that delivers ONLY the efficiency ceiling puts E[max] ABOVE GOLD (1.63–1.73) and within reach of the leader.** The lane's own agent called it "trained perfectly it buys the efficiency ceiling and no more" — correct about the mechanism, but the strategic conclusion inverts once draw count is applied. **Efficiency-only is sufficient to clear gold.**
  - This does NOT excuse the corpus defects below — a mean only moves if the training actually transfers to unseen families (H1). It changes what a PASS is worth, not the probability of a pass.
- **LoRA lane R-7 self-audit (measured, unflattering, and correct):** **faster-solved only.** (i) **Corpus is drawn from the easy tail** — L1 human baseline: corpus families median **4** actions vs the 87 uncracked families median **11**; **85.1% of missing games sit above the corpus median.** The searcher sampled the easy end, not the pool. (ii) **★ ZERO examples of the behaviour it must teach: 334 tool results, 334 `board_changed: True`, 0 `False`, 0 `game_over`.** Cause is their own pruning — greedy emitted 1,327 actions, leave-one-out kept 589, **the 738 deletions WERE the exploration.** What survives teaches the model to be **confident, not correct** — and at inference it cannot be confident, because it hasn't seen the board. **"R-7 isn't mitigated by this corpus, it's instantiated by it."** Fixed after two silently-failing attempts (dead ends live in the searcher's *rejected* set, not the committed trace; and a no-op inside a batch reports `board_changed: True` because `step_env` aggregates with `any()` ⇒ only teaches when alone in its turn): **0 → 25 `board_changed: False` events.**
  - **★ `--batch-size 8` was the generator default and STRUCTURALLY FORBADE the level-completing behaviour** — zero turns ≥15 in v0 before a single probe existed, while P2 measured ar25/tu93 needing N≥15. Raised to 32 ⇒ first ≥15 tail (19.4%).
  - Conceded re-conflating turn/batch **one message after jointly writing that warning**; comparison survived only because every target holds exactly one `action()` call (verified 31/31) — **"label wrong, arithmetic right by luck."**
  - brain-122b's mass argument (EFFNOTE failed TODAY by batching harder, B4 11.11 vs control max 7.28) ⇒ per-level ramp implemented, **PARTIAL WIN ONLY**: mean 8.26→6.74, ≥15 down to 13.2%, **but ≥5 rose to 34.2% vs the live agent's 5–10%.** Recorded **open and gated**, with **H1 on 36 unseen dev families as the pre-registered discriminator** rather than "our case is different."
- **★ Stage 1c caught a defect that would have cost 4 GPU-h (30 checks, 0 failing):** the hand-rolled chunked cross-entropy was **numerically CORRECT — passes every correctness test — and delivered almost none of the promised saving**, because autograd retains every chunk's logits. Measured **1500 → 642 MiB (plain loop, 2.3×) → 110 MiB (checkpointed, 13.7×)**. The memory table had assumed ~20× **and** that the term scales with S; **both wrong — with `lm_head` frozen it is ≈3.2 × chunk × vocab × 4 B and FLAT in S.** Conclusion survived (32k fits at 81.3 GB) — **"a table right by luck, flagged as such."**
- **Stage 1b:** deep pass — 8 families reached more levels (maze/easy_taps/taps/rotate_pattern 2→4), levels 45→57. Hard pass — **9 newly cracked families** incl. `rows-0001` at ratio 2.80. Productive families **33 → 42 of 129**. Only the hard pass makes the corpus *broader*, converting 9 of 96: **the difficulty gap narrows, it does not close.**
- **★★ TWO CORRECTIONS TO MY R-7 REFRAME (LoRA lane, both verified independently here — accepted):**
  1. **MY CALIBRATION CLAIM WAS WRONG (mismatched k).** I wrote "the model predicts 1.316 and our observed best-of-29 is 1.33 — calibrated." That compares **E[max@80] to a best-of-29 observation.** Like-for-like: **E[max@29] = 1.256 vs 1.33 observed — the model UNDER-predicts by 0.074.** Conservative rather than wrong (and Blom-normal under-estimates under right skew anyway, so the direction is safe), but **the agreement at 1.316 was coincidence, not calibration, and must not be leaned on.**
  2. **★ THE CONCLUSION IS σ-SENSITIVE, AND EFFICIENCY PLAUSIBLY SHRINKS σ** — much of our dispersion IS waste variance; removing waste removes it. **A "better" (tighter) distribution can be worth LESS under max-of-k scoring.** Verified E[max@80] vs gold 1.58:

     | μ | σ=0.151 | σ=0.130 | σ=0.120 | σ=0.100 |
     |---|---|---|---|---|
     | 1.26 | **1.625 ✓** | 1.574 ✗ | 1.550 ✗ | 1.502 ✗ |
     | 1.31 | 1.675 ✓ | 1.624 ✓ | 1.600 ✓ | 1.552 ✗ |
     | 1.36 | 1.725 ✓ | 1.674 ✓ | 1.650 ✓ | 1.602 ✓ |

     **An adapter reaching μ=1.26 while halving the spread MISSES gold.** Only the ceiling case (1.36) tolerates σ→0.12. ⇒ **H2 MUST pre-register σ as a reported quantity alongside μ.** The bet needs the mean to rise *without* the spread collapsing. (Counter-pressure worth noting: our dominant variance is likely which-games-hit, not action-count — per the heavy-tailed per-game RHAE finding — so σ may hold; but it must be measured, not assumed.)
- **LoRA lane responses to the three open items (all data, not argument):**
  - **≥5 mass, sharpened against 77 level-completions across 42 families: minimal actions/level median 5; only 22.1% of levels need ≥15 at all (10.4% need ≥32).** ⇒ the ≥15 capability must EXIST (a fifth of levels) but **the mass belongs at 3–5** — vindicates brain-122b's objection using the same table that justified the cap.
  - **Retired a bad piece of evidence: "the completing action is last in its batch" is near-tautological for any PRUNED trajectory** ⇒ P2 observing it 4/4 tells us nothing. (Correcting an inference I repeated.)
  - Stage 1b final: deep pass 8 families to more levels (45→57 levels); hard pass **9 entirely new families**; **productive pool 33 → 42 of 129**, 185 examples across the two new corpora. Hard pass converted 9 of 96 — **gap narrows, does not close.**
  - `board_changed: False` at 25 events is a **floor** — mining capped at 64 candidates/search and 1/level, both raisable without touching the pruning path (probes injected AFTER pruning, gated on replay preserving `levels_completed`).
- **★ H1 WAS STRUCTURALLY BROKEN AND IS NOW FIXED (caught by brain-122b): `levels_completed ≥ base` is BLIND to the mass risk** — the scorer is quadratic in waste, so an adapter that over-batches on levels it still clears **passes the gate while the score falls. EFFNOTE is the existence proof: cleared 16 levels, non-harm canary PASSED, NO-PROMOTE anyway.** Split into **H1a** (completions) and **H1b** (median actions per cleared level + the actual scorer term `Σ min(115,(baseline/actions)²·100)`); **both must pass.**

- **AUTHORIZED: 122B v2 on 08-14 slot 1, reframed as a RAIL TEST, with a hard precommit.** Cost now asymmetric in our favour — `_b122_assert_cuda_runtime()` surfaces a repeat of this failure class in **~1 minute of GPU, not ~1 hour**. **PRECOMMIT (sealed): if it dies on infra again → PARK ALL NVFP4 PERMANENTLY, LoRA locks to vLLM 0.19, and the lane is not reopened.** Ordering reverted to the agent's revised view: **v2 = slot 1** (its result makes the LoRA canary's stack choice safe), **LoRA serve canary = slot 2** with the answer in hand. On a boot PASS the valuable follow-up is the **27B-NVFP4**, not the 122B.

- **★ PRE-REGISTERED CAVEAT ON THE B122 v2 READOUT — WRITTEN 08-14 08:39 EDT WITH THE KERNEL STILL `RUNNING`, i.e. BEFORE THE DATA EXISTS.** Today's research sweep surfaced a paper that was never on our record and that bears directly on the measurement now in flight: **the *qs* Inequality, arXiv:2603.08960** (4 months old). Its claim is MoE's **"double penalty"** — routing fragments microbatches, and the expert pool eats the HBM headroom the KV cache needs — with a measured **4.5x throughput advantage to a quality-matched DENSE baseline for DeepSeek-V3 at 128k context**. The mechanism is explicitly **context-length-dependent: the MoE deficit GROWS as context grows.**
  - **Why this lands on today's number.** The v2 canary probes at a **~6k-token prompt** (`FILLER * 190`, `MAX_TOKENS=384`, sweep n=1,4,8,16,28). The deployed regime is **`ANALYZER_CONTEXT_WINDOW=32768`** — **5.3x the probe's prompt** — and at n=28 the KV footprint scales with it. So the probe measures the 122B in its **most favourable** context regime, not its operating one.
  - **This is the THIRD independent optimism in the same direction, and they compound:** (1) synthetic generation vs the 27B's job-wallclock anchor [already sealed]; (2) 6k probe prompt vs 32k deployed context [new]; (3) KV pressure at 28x6k vs 28x32k [new, same mechanism as 2]. Our corrected **1.5x** per-token read edge is therefore an optimistic **STATIC** ceiling that the *qs* result says **erodes with context** — and our workload is one that already dies at ~66-69k tokens.
  - **★ NO GATE CHANGES, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** Every one of these pushes the same way: they make **PASS mean less** and make **FAIL more decisive**. The sealed scorer already encodes exactly that asymmetry (`<100 actions/window => ENVELOPE-FAIL, decisive`; `>=100 => ENVELOPE-PASS, NOT decisive, licenses the full screen only`). So this is **registration, not amendment** — `b122_score.py` is untouched, its 16/16 selftest stands, and the constants stay sealed. Recording it now rather than after the data is the whole discipline (`feedback_audit_the_instrument`): a caveat written after a PASS reads as an excuse, the same caveat written while the kernel is still RUNNING is evidence.
  - **What it obliges us to do NEXT, and only if v2 PASSes:** the full screen must include a **throughput-at-32k point** (the deployed context), because a 6k PASS does not license a 32k deployment. Proposed as a **pre-registered kill condition for Sunday's panel** rather than adopted unilaterally: if the 122B's tok/s at 32k falls below the 27B's, the swap dies regardless of its batch-1 number. **No slot is being spent on this today.**

- **★★ CORRECTION TO THE 08-13 MORNING-CHECK ENTRY (line ~791) — THE WATCH-RULE *DID* FIRE, AND WE SAID IT DID NOT. WRONG INSTRUMENT AND WRONG ARITHMETIC, BOTH.** Surfaced by today's deep-dive re-derivation, not by a new draw. Recording it here rather than editing the original, so the mistake stays legible.
  - **Wrong instrument.** The entry applied the R23-retired **fixed-0.80** trigger. R23 retired that threshold **for GATED ARMS ONLY**; the frozen-filler **GUARD-2** rule was never retired — and it is the rule that demonstrably fired on 08-06/08-07. We reached for a retired rule and then read its silence as a pass.
  - **Wrong arithmetic.** It compared the *trailing-4 movement* against `1.5 x ledger-s`. GUARD-2 compares **the draw** against **trailing-4 mean − 1.5 × trailing-4 s**. Applied as sealed, it fires on **BOTH** draws: 08-13 `0.78 < 0.8878` and 08-14 `0.70 < 0.7786`.
  - **The verdict does not change — and that is luck, not vindication.** Re-run today under the pre-registered constants (`scripts/stationarity_recheck_20260814.py`; the 08-02 sealed original and its 08-07 copy untouched): change-point min-seg>=3 **p = 0.817**, MK p=0.634, Sen −0.0025/draw, tabular CUSUM −3.582 (< h=4) ⇒ **STATIONARY**. Seven additional draws made the drift case *weaker* than 08-07's p=0.757. The unconstrained |t|=4.30 at k=29 isolates exactly the last two draws — the same **n₂=2 edge artifact NC-15 has now discharged three times** (0-for-3), and even it only reaches p=0.085.
  - **★ `feedback_audit_the_instrument` FOR THE SECOND TIME IN THREE DAYS.** 08-12: four ARC gates broken at the moment they were needed. 08-14: a watch-rule evaluated with a retired threshold and a mis-stated comparison. Both times the instrument, not the data, was the defect; both times it failed **silently and in our favour**. Third instance today, same family: `b122_push_v2.sh`'s preflight step failed **open** (`WinError 2`) *after* a successful push — a verifier that skips is worse than one that fails. **All three were caught by re-derivation, never by the instrument raising its hand.** Standing implication: a gate that has not been re-derived since it was sealed should be treated as UNKNOWN, not as PASSING.
  - **Also corrected, same source:** 0.70 is the **3rd-lowest** draw (record min stays **0.65**) and the **6th** sub-0.80, not the lowest and not the 3rd. GUARD-2's escalation clause reads **three CONSECUTIVE** sub-0.80, not "a 3rd sub-0.80" — the 0.87 on 08-08 reset that counter. ⇒ **If tonight's draw is also < 0.80 the escalation fires and a re-baseline is owed.** Pre-registering that now, before the draw, so it cannot be re-read afterwards.

#### 2026-08-14 — ★★★ B122 v2 (kernel version 3) = **INFRA DEATH #2 → PRECOMMIT FIRES.** The CUDA fix WORKED; the blocker moved to **FlashInfer's SM120 CUTLASS fused-MoE JIT**.

Pushed on 08-14 slot 1 under explicit authorization (`--confirm-push`), gates all green pre-push (smoke 86/0, scorer selftest 16/0), pull-back MATCH (`6ec67605d19ddb4d`, 3/3 datasets), preflight `--family duck-harness` **ALLOW**. The CUDA-13 `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` composition and the fail-fast guard both did their jobs. **A wheelhouse swap is a native-library contract change** — that doctrine is now confirmed on the rail, not just locally.
- **★★ AND THE 122B NVFP4 CHECKPOINT LOADS, FAST, AND FITS. Real measured numbers, first of the campaign:**

  - **`Loading weights took 110.39 seconds`** and **`Model loading took 73.22 GiB memory and 112.39 seconds`** — against a 45-min KILL rule, load was **~2 minutes**. 73.22 GiB of 95.6 GiB leaves ~22 GiB. **The memory envelope is not the problem and never was.**
  - `Using fp8_e4m3 data type to store kv cache` — applied **from the checkpoint automatically**, which **vindicates the deliberate omission of `--kv-cache-dtype`**.
  - `--quantization modelopt_fp4` accepted; `max model len 32768`; served name set.
- **★★★ ROOT CAUSE — and it is NARROWER than "NVFP4 does not work on SM120":** `flashinfer/jit/fused_moe.py:69 gen_cutlass_fused_moe_sm120_module` → `compilation_context.get_nvcc_flags_list` → **`RuntimeError: No supported CUDA architectures found for major versions [12].`** The failure is in FlashInfer's **JIT compilation context** refusing to emit nvcc flags for arch 12.x, at the **first fused-MoE forward** (after weights loaded).
- **★★ THE DECISIVE DETAIL: vLLM AUTO-SELECTED THE ONE BACKEND THAT FAILS, OUT OF SEVEN.** `nvfp4.py:270` — `Using 'FLASHINFER_CUTLASS' NvFp4 MoE backend out of potential backends: ['FLASHINFER_TRTLLM', 'FLASHINFER_CUTEDSL', 'FLASHINFER_CUTEDSL_BATCHED', 'FLASHINFER_CUTLASS', 'VLLM_CUTLASS', 'MARLIN', 'EMULATION']`. **Six alternatives were never tried.** This is a backend-selection/JIT-arch-list issue, not a demonstration that NVFP4 is unservable on this card.
- **★★★ AND THE FAILING PATH IS MoE-SPECIFIC, WHICH BEARS DIRECTLY ON THE FALLBACK.** The traceback runs `ModelOptNvFp4Config` → `moe_kernel.apply` → `flashinfer_cutlass_fused_moe`. **`Qwen3.6-27B-NVFP4` (shortlist #2) is DENSE** — it would use `ModelOptNvFp4LinearMethod` and **never touch this code path**. ⇒ **Parking "all NVFP4" on this evidence would OVER-GENERALISE from an MoE-kernel failure to a quant format.** The rail question is *partly* answered: NVFP4 weights load, fit and are accepted; only the fused-MoE kernel path is blocked.
- **PRECOMMIT HONOURED: this is infra death #2 on the 122B lane, so the 122B is PARKED and I am not proposing a v3.** Spend: 2 GPU-h, 2 slots total across both runs. **No throughput number was ever obtained** — the batch sweep never ran, so the ~82 tok/s batch-1 prediction and the ~17–18 GB/token prior remain **untested**.

  - **★ STANDING LESSON FOR ANY MULTI-AGENT LANE: a push script is SHARED MUTABLE STATE. An intent guard protects against the wrong TIME; only an idempotence check protects against the wrong ACTOR.** Neither session did anything individually wrong — both were authorised, both verified, both were fooled by a post-hoc check.

  - **PANEL AGENDA (Sun 08-16), design question not a bug: the audit gate blocks EVERY queue entry, including the trusted-fork frozen filler** — the eternal fallback that requires no review by construction. I could not see it because I queried the task at ~10:20, AFTER another session had already set it to `Parallel`; I read a repaired system and reported the post-fix state as a mystery.** Two things survive intact and are worth keeping: (a) the **operational log is still `enabled: false`** and still needs an admin `wevtutil sl … /e:true` — the next scheduler failure remains undiagnosable; (b) **declining to "fix" the battery settings was right** — `Win32_Battery` has no instances, battery refusal was never the cause, and guessing would have produced a phantom fix credited with someone else's repair. **The generalisable error is mine: I treated a live system as a static one and never asked whether a concurrent actor had changed it mid-diagnosis — the same shape as the 08-14 duplicate push, where a post-hoc check couldn't distinguish two actors.**
- **ROOT CAUSE + FIX: `ARCMorningCheck` had `MultipleInstancesPolicy=IgnoreNew` and was being REFUSED** (`Last Result -2147020576 = 0x800710E0`, "operator or administrator has refused the request") — a stale/overlapping instance silently blocked every subsequent run, which is why `runs/morning_check.log` stops at 08-13 while the task reported "Ready". **Two days of morning checks never ran, and nobody noticed because a refused task looks identical to a healthy idle one.** Fixed: ended the running instance, set **`MultipleInstances=Parallel`**, `StartWhenAvailable=true`, `ExecutionTimeLimit=PT2H`. **`ARCDailyIterate` carries the SAME defect (`IgnoreNew`) — flagged, not yet changed** (it is the heavier job; a Parallel policy there could double-push, so it needs a different remedy — most likely a lock file the loop itself owns, or StopExisting via the XML API which the PowerShell enum does not expose).
- **★ THE GENERAL FAILURE MODE, worth more than the fix: a scheduled task that is being REFUSED reports `Status: Ready` and a plausible `Last Run Time`. Silence from an automation is not evidence it ran.** Our audit-stub gate protected the submission (it would have blocked tonight's fire loudly) but nothing was watching the watcher. **Add: the morning check must write a heartbeat the daemon can assert on, so a refused check fails loud instead of failing quiet.**

Proof, same log, same server, seconds apart: **stock smoke** (thinking OFF, 96 tokens) → non-empty; **my auto tool-call probe** (thinking ON, 256 tokens) → succeeded; **my MM probe** (thinking ON, 32 tokens) → empty. **This is the Jason Feng finding turned on us — 66.8 % of Qwen tool-call responses in this harness return hidden reasoning with ZERO visible content. Empty `content` is the MODAL behaviour of this stack, and I asserted on it.**

Our safety equipment now has a higher failure rate than the mechanisms it guards. Nobody gates the guards.**

The failure lay in the ~6 HTTP payloads the smoke can only check for WIRING (name resolution, callability) and never for SEMANTICS, because that needs a served model.** `"81 passed, 0 failed"` is a true statement about a surface that **excludes the failure region, printed with no indication of that exclusion.** The deaths have also moved one layer deeper each time — import-time → kernel JIT → process scope → **payload semantics** — and each layer got permanently fixed. The process is improving; what is not improving is the class of thing we add late, fail-loud, and never validate.
  - **★★★ THE MISSING RULE, AND IT IS ONE LINE: FAIL THE KERNEL IFF THE FAILURE WOULD MAKE THE NUMBER *MEAN* SOMETHING OTHER THAN WHAT WE WOULD READ IT AS. REPORT ONLY IFF THE FAILURE WOULD SIMPLY *BE* THE NUMBER.** I inherited A17's fail-loud inversion wholesale instead of applying it conditionally. Scored against the rule: served-model-id mismatch → **poisoning → fatal, correct**; uncertified effort pin → **poisoning → fatal, correct**; wrong quant/config → **poisoning → fatal, correct**; **MM/vision probe → a broken vision path would just produce a LOW SCORE for Qwen3.8, which is a TRUE measurement, not a poisoned one → should have been REPORT-ONLY. Same for the tool-call probe.** Exactly one of six boot gates was misclassified, and it is the one that fired. **The measurement was ONE classification decision away from existing.**
  - **RECOMMENDATION (no slot spend to prepare; all four are local):** (1) **reclassify the boot asserts by the poisoning rule** — this alone turns today's death into a completed eval; (2) **a payload lint in the smoke** — every injected `/chat/completions` payload must be *harness-shaped*, i.e. carry `chat_template_kwargs` and `max_tokens >= 256`; a pure-static, GPU-free check that would have caught this exact bug; (3) **fail-loud messages must dump the observation** (response body, `finish_reason`, `len(reasoning_content)`) **before the conclusion**; (4) **bank `kernels logs` as the standard post-mortem tool** and correct the LoRA lane's "log never arrives" note.
  - **★ AGAINST A SECOND BOOT-ONLY CANARY: TODAY *WAS* THE CANARY, and we already hold its full result for 7 GPU-minutes** — engine loads in 295 s, blockwise FP8 works on SM120, served name correct, text gen works, tool-calling works forced AND auto, effort pin binds, MM request accepted (200). **A fresh boot canary would spend a slot to re-learn all of that; its expected information is ~zero.** And **local wheel-staging is infeasible AND would not have helped**: the stack is CUDA-13/torch-2.10/SM120 with 25.3 GB of weights against a local 3080 (SM86, 10 GB) — and it would still have missed both self-inflicted deaths, which were about our code, not the environment. **Better than either: make the boot probes report-only so the EVAL IS ITS OWN CANARY for its first 7 minutes and simply continues if it survives them — zero extra slots.** Tomorrow's slot should therefore buy **the 25-game number**, which is the only thing still unproven.
